id: toolu_01BJ8bKYZC873vfNhTva2yG9
```
#!/bin/bash

set -euo pipefail

GITHUB_REPO="entireio/cli"
DEFAULT_INSTALL_DIR="$HOME/.local/bin"

# Colors (disabled in non-interactive mode)
if [[ -t 1 ]]; then
    RED='\033[0;31m'
    GREEN='\033[0;32m'
    YELLOW='\033[0;33m'
    BLUE='\033[0;34m'
    BOLD='\033[1m'
    NC='\033[0m' # No Color
else
    RED=''
    GREEN=''
    YELLOW=''
    BLUE=''
    BOLD=''
    NC=''
fi

info() {
    printf '%b%s%b\n' "${BLUE}==>${NC} ${BOLD}" "$1" "${NC}"
}

success() {
    printf '%b%s%b\n' "${GREEN}==>${NC} ${BOLD}" "$1" "${NC}"
}

warn() {
    printf '%b %s\n' "${YELLOW}Warning:${NC}" "$1"
}

error() {
    printf '%b %s\n' "${RED}Error:${NC}" "$1" >&2
    exit 1
}

detect_os() {
    local os
    os="$(uname -s | tr '[:upper:]' '[:lower:]')"
    case "$os" in
        darwin)
            echo "darwin"
            ;;
        linux)
            echo "linux"
```

```
# Track B: Balanced (12h cycle, 12h duration)
# Track C: Deep (24h cycle, 24h duration)
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/scripts/update_tools.sh",
  "content": "#!/bin/bash\n# update_tools.sh - Daily update checker for all Cohezion CLI tools\n# Run manually or via systemd timer (cohezion-tools-update.timer)\n# Logs to: logs/tool_updates.log\n\nset -euo pipefail\n\nLOG_FILE=\"${HOME}/dev/cohezion/logs/tool_updates.log\"\nTIMESTAMP=$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')\nUPDATED=()\nFAILED=()\nSKIPPED=()\n\nlog() { echo \"[$TIMESTAMP] $*\" | tee -a \"$LOG_FILE\"; }\nlog_ok() { echo \"[$TIMESTAMP]  $*\" | tee -a \"$LOG_FILE\"; }\nlog_err() { echo \"[$TIMESTAMP] ✗ $*\" | tee -a \"$LOG_FILE\"; }\n\nlog \"=== Tool Update Check ===\"\n\n# ── entire ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_entire() {\n    local current latest\n    current=$(entire version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    latest=$(curl -sf https://api.github.com/repos/entireio/cli/releases/latest | grep -oP '\"tag_name\":\\s*\"v\\K[^\"]+' || echo \"\")\n\n    if [[ -z \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        log_err \"entire: could not fetch latest version\"\n        FAILED+=(\"entire\")\n        return\n    fi\n\n    if [[ \"$current\" == \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        log_ok \"entire: already at v$current\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"entire\")\n    else\n        log \"entire: updating v$current → v$latest\"\n        if curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash >> \"$LOG_FILE\" 2>&1; then\n            log_ok \"entire: updated to v$latest\"\n            UPDATED+=(\"entire v$current→v$latest\")\n        else\n            log_err \"entire: update failed\"\n            FAILED+=(\"entire\")\n        fi\n    fi\n}\n\n# ── sx ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_sx() {\n    local current\n    current=$(sx --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    log \"sx: checking (current v$current)\"\n    local output\n    if output=$(sx update 2>&1); then\n        if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"already\"; then\n            log_ok \"sx: already up to date (v$current)\"\n            SKIPPED+=(\"sx\")\n        else\n            local new_ver\n            new_ver=$(sx --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"?\")\n            log_ok \"sx: updated v$current → v$new_ver\"\n            UPDATED+=(\"sx v$current→v$new_ver\")\n        fi\n    else\n        log_err \"sx: update failed\"\n        FAILED+=(\"sx\")\n    fi\n}\n\n# ── uv ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_uv() {\n    local current\n    current=$(uv --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    log \"uv: checking (current v$current)\"\n    if uv self update >> \"$LOG_FILE\" 2>&1; then\n        local new_ver\n        new_ver=$(uv --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"?\")\n        if [[ \"$current\" == \"$new_ver\" ]]; then\n            log_ok \"uv: already at v$current\"\n            SKIPPED+=(\"uv\")\n        else\n            log_ok \"uv: updated v$current → v$new_ver\"\n            UPDATED+=(\"uv v$current→v$new_ver\")\n        fi\n    else\n        log_err \"uv: update failed\"\n        FAILED+=(\"uv\")\n    fi\n}\n\n# ── gh ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_gh() {\n    local current latest\n    current=$(gh --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    latest=$(curl -sf https://api.github.com/repos/cli/cli/releases/latest | grep -oP '\"tag_name\":\\s*\"v\\K[^\"]+' || echo \"\")\n\n    if [[ -z \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        log_err \"gh: could not fetch latest version\"\n        FAILED+=(\"gh\")\n        return\n    fi\n\n    if [[ \"$current\" == \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        log_ok \"gh: already at v$current\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"gh\")\n    else\n        log \"gh: updating v$current → v$latest\"\n        local url=\"https://github.com/cli/cli/releases/download/v${latest}/gh_${latest}_linux_amd64.tar.gz\"\n        local tmp\n        tmp=$(mktemp -d)\n        if curl -fsSL \"$url\" | tar -xz -C \"$tmp\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" 2>&1; then\n            install -m 755 \"$tmp/gh_${latest}_linux_amd64/bin/gh\" \"${HOME}/.local/bin/gh\"\n            rm -rf \"$tmp\"\n            log_ok \"gh: updated to v$latest\"\n            UPDATED+=(\"gh v$current→v$latest\")\n        else\n            rm -rf \"$tmp\"\n            log_err \"gh: update failed\"\n            FAILED+=(\"gh\")\n        fi\n    fi\n}\n\n# ── claude ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_claude() {\n    local current latest\n    current=$(claude --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    latest=$(curl -sf https://api.github.com/repos/anthropics/claude-code/releases/latest 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '\"tag_name\":\\s*\"v?\\K[^\"]+' || echo \"\")\n\n    if [[ -z \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        # Claude auto-updates on first run; log current and skip\n        log_ok \"claude: at v$current (self-managed updates)\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"claude\")\n        return\n    fi\n\n    if [[ \"$current\" == \"$latest\" ]]; then\n        log_ok \"claude: already at v$current\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"claude\")\n    else\n        log \"claude: new version v$latest available (current v$current) — run 'claude' to auto-update\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"claude (manual: run claude to update)\")\n    fi\n}\n\n# ── pilot ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_pilot() {\n    if ! command -v pilot &>/dev/null; then\n        log_ok \"pilot: not in PATH, skipping\"\n        SKIPPED+=(\"pilot\")\n        return\n    fi\n    local current\n    current=$(pilot --version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1 || echo \"unknown\")\n    log_ok \"pilot: at v$current (managed by Pilot installer)\"\n    SKIPPED+=(\"pilot\")\n}\n\n# ── Run all updates ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nupdate_entire\nupdate_sx\nupdate_uv\nupdate_gh\nupdate_claude\nupdate_pilot\n\n# ── Summary ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nlog \"\"\nlog \"=== Summary ===\"\nif [[ ${#UPDATED[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then\n    log_ok \"Updated: ${UPDATED[*]}\"\nfi\nif [[ ${#SKIPPED[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then\n    log_ok \"Up to date: ${SKIPPED[*]}\"\nfi\nif [[ ${#FAILED[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then\n    log_err \"Failed: ${FAILED[*]}\"\n    exit 1\nfi\nlog \"Done.\"\n"
}
```

Key insights: barrier-locked manifolds prevent temporal instruction leakage; batch processing inside Rust (via rayon) amortizes FFI overhead for 29x speedup over naive 1:1 calls; windowing provides the largest single performance jump after SIMD.

    28→Stability through mortality — introducing apoptosis and mitosis forces dynamic equilibrium (HIHO state). Immortal agents stagnate.

    31→Cognitive specialization > parameters. Routed swarm of domain experts (DeepSeek-R1-8B, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B, Phi4-Mini) outperforms generic 7B model.
    32→
    33→## Learning 20: VRAM Persistence & The Sudo Trap
    34→Automated recovery must never rely on `sudo`. Use direct Ollama `/api/generate` with `keep_alive: 0` and AMD `/sys` telemetry for non-privileged VRAM management.

    37→Refinement over generation — agentic reasoning shifts from next-token prediction to state-machine planning with recursive verification, checkpointing, and Merkle indexing.

    39→## Learning 26: The Python Autoregression Bottleneck
    40→GIL limits autoregressive decoding to ~10Hz. Inference loops must move to compiled languages (Rust/C++) for 100Hz+ fluid behavior.
    41→
    42→## Learning 27: Rust FFI Bridge Success
    43→PyO3 + maturin + uv provides seamless Rust-Python bridge. Critical: ensure LD_LIBRARY_PATH/PYTHONPATH for shared object linking during testing.
    44→
    45→## Learning 28: FFI Overhead & The Batching Pivot
    46→Naive 1:1 FFI calls = 0.2x speedup (regression). Moving iteration inside Rust with rayon = 29.1x speedup (20.45s → 0.70s for 10k items).
    47→
    48→## Learning 29: Semantic Proprioception
    49→Intent over vitals — projecting system state into 12D latent manifold detects "Logic Drift" that simple thresholding misses. 0.63 coherence alignment achieved.
    50→
    51→## Learning 30: The 3-Beat Actuation Law
    52→Require 3 consecutive low-coherence beats before triggering repair. Single-point anomalies are noise.
    53→
    54→## Learning 41: The Filesystem Entropy Limit
    55→Filesystems >1M files incur "Entropy Tax" paralyzing IDE indexers. Cold storage isolation (`.archive/`) + SurrealDB persistence is the solution.
    56→
    57→## Learning 42: ZFS Sovereign Swap
    58→ZVOL (32GB) bypasses ZFS COW incompatibility with swap files. Secured 40GB OOM protection buffer.

    61→Hard cap `zfs_arc_max` to 12.5% of RAM (16GB) prevents filesystem from starving AI models.

    67→HIHO attractor (0.5) stable at 25M cycles. Convergence follows damped oscillation: C(t) = 0.5 + A·e^(-kt)·sin(ωt).

    79→Shared `ConnectionPool` with `httpx.AsyncClient` reduces socket overhead >80%. Tri-state circuit breaker (Closed/Open/Half-Open) prevents cascading latency.

    85→`mem_info_vram_total` reports 512MB carveout (always ~88% full — it's display scanout). Real pool is `mem_info_gtt_total` (128GB). Discriminator: if vram_total < 4GB, use GTT instead.
    86→
    87→## Learning 92: Adaptive AMD iGPU Detection via Sysfs (2026-02-05)
    88→Vendor `0x1002` = AMD. Prefer GTT over VRAM path. If GTT within 5% of system RAM → UMA. Scoring: `vram_score = min(system_ram_gb / 64.0, 2.0)`.
    89→
    90→## Learning 93: JSON Comment Stripping (Config Resilience) (2026-02-05)
    91→Strip `#` comment lines before `json.loads()`. Never silently return empty dict on parse failure.
    92→
    93→## Learning 94: Lazy Import Chains (Dependency Firewall) (2026-02-05)
    94→Move imports to point-of-use to create a dependency firewall. Add `# noqa: E402` to prevent ruff from hoisting them back.
    95→
    96→## Learning 95: End-to-End Pipeline Verification (2026-02-05)
    97→Pipeline health depends on a chain of 4 correct subsystems (sysfs read → monitor → router → agent). Any single failure cascades. 5-stage integration test protocol catches compound failures.
    98→
    99→---
   100→

   123→## Phase 1-2 Milestones (2026-02-06)
   124→
   125→### FLUME VAE Trained on Real Data
   126→Mass sim exported 10 universes × 100 agents × 500 epochs → 61 .npy files, 11K vectors. VAE retrained: MSE 0.0225→0.1322 (5.9x harder real data), KL 0.0313→0.4329 (13.8x richer latent). Real distributions are far more complex than synthetic.

   130→
   131→### Mass Sim → .npy Export Pipeline

   136→
   137→## Learning 96: Agent File Validation as Compound Defense (2026-02-06)
   138→Missing YAML frontmatter in `.claude/agents/*.md` files was only caught by `claude doctor` after the fact. Fix: single Pydantic schema (`validation/agent_schema.py`) shared by pre-commit hook, PostToolUse hook, unit tests, and `/new-agent` scaffolding command. Layered defense = catch at commit time, warn in real-time, scaffold valid by default. Compound engineering: each layer reuses the same schema.
   139→
   140→---
   141→
   142→## Phase 3: Ollama-Powered Specialist Pipeline (2026-02-06)
   143→
   144→### Learning 97: Weight Bridge Layer Collapse
   145→PolicyNetwork has 3 linear layers but Rust FlumePhysics accepts only 2. Collapse intermediate layer via matrix multiplication: `w2 = mean_head.weight @ shared[2].weight`, `b2 = mean_head.bias + mean_head.weight @ shared[2].bias`. LayerNorm defaults: gamma=ones, beta=0.5 (HIHO target). Note: `shared[2]` is the second Linear in nn.Sequential (index 0=Linear, 1=ReLU, 2=Linear, 3=ReLU).
   146→
   147→### Learning 98: Ruff Hook Import Fights
   148→PostToolUse ruff hook auto-removes "unused" imports. Conditional usage is not recognized as "used." Fix: use the import in a type annotation (`self._exporter: CheckpointExporter | None`) which ruff always considers "used."
   149→
   150→### Learning 99: Deterministic Navigator for Simulation
   151→For stable mass sim, use deterministic mean action from PolicyNetwork (no Gaussian sampling), scaled by action_scale. Sampling introduces too much noise for batch simulation with thousands of agents.
   152→
   153→### Learning 100: Democratic Debate for Structured Output
   154→DemocraticDebate extracts numeric hyperparameters from free-text consensus via regex. Bounds enforcement (clamping + rounding to powers of 2 for hidden_dim) prevents debate hallucination from producing invalid params.
   155→
   156→### Learning 101: Pipeline Graceful Degradation
   157→9-step bash pipeline with pre-flight checks. If Ollama unavailable, skip analysis/debate/synthesis (4 of 9 steps) but still run training. Scale-specific params via case statement (demo/medium/overnight).
   158→
   159→---
   160→
   161→## Branch Archaeology: The Runaway Files Incident (2026-01-25 — 2026-01-30)
   162→
   163→Mined from `fix/runaway-files-pre-cleanup` and `ops/hygiene` branches before cleanup.
   164→
   165→### Learning 102: The 8.6M Runaway File Catastrophe (2026-01-25)
   166→Autonomous overnight simulations generated 8.6 million files under `data/overnight/`, `results/`, `renders/`, and `src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/`. Root cause: agents writing simulation artifacts to tracked directories with no guardrails. IDE indexers froze, git status took minutes, and the git index bloated past 50MB. Prevention: (1) `.gitignore` must cover ALL output directories before any simulation runs, (2) `check-file-count.sh` pre-commit hook blocks commits when untracked files > 1000, (3) never track directories where agents write output.
   167→
   168→### Learning 103: Cascading Cleanup Debt (2026-01-26)
   169→The first cleanup (7c6d60a) removed 8.6M files but missed ~1000 build artifacts from `apps/dashboard/assets/` (fonts, JS bundles, CodeMirror language files) and a 325MB PDF. Second pass (1c07dcc) caught these by adding `apps/dashboard/assets/` and `**/*.safetensors` to .gitignore. Lesson: cleanup is never one-pass. Each removal reveals the next layer of bloat. Budget 2-3 passes minimum.
   170→
   171→### Learning 104: System Lockup Pattern — GPU Hang (2026-01-27)
   172→Total system freeze during concurrent web apps + LLM swarm. Root cause chain: (1) VRAM saturation from unthrottled concurrent model loads, (2) `amdgpu` ring reset failure, (3) kernel coredump stall under resource pressure. Required REISUB recovery. Fix: direct sysfs GPU/VRAM monitoring, aggressive PID kill in RED alert, `tune_system.sh` to disable panic-on-oom, prevention of giant coredumps. Key insight: **VRAM is the bottleneck, not RAM. Swarms must be sacrificial; system integrity is primary.**
   173→
   174→### Learning 105: The Untrack & Mine Protocol
   175→Operational procedure invented during ops/hygiene: (1) identify tracked files that shouldn't be, (2) mine them for knowledge before removal, (3) add to .gitignore, (4) `git rm --cached`, (5) verify git status clean. Critical rule: NEVER delete without reading first. This protocol prevented knowledge loss during the 8.6M file cleanup.
   176→
   177→### Learning 106: .gitignore Layered Defense Pattern
   178→The .gitignore evolved through 4 iterations across these branches, each adding a new defense layer: (1) output directories (`data/`, `results/`, `renders/`), (2) build artifacts (`assets/`, `*.safetensors`), (3) binary patterns (`*.pt`, `*.pdf`, `*.so`, `*.mp3`), (4) negation rules to protect source (`!src/**/*.py`, `!scripts/**/*.py`). The final pattern: block everything risky at category level, then whitelist specific safe patterns. Order matters — negations must come after the block rule.
   179→
   180→### Learning 107: OMEGA Distiller — Skill Extraction from Success Logs
   181→Auto-skill-generation concept: parse "MISSION SUCCESS" logs, strip noise/timestamps/paths, extract the "trick" (specific insight that turned failure into success), generalize into PRIME skill format. Template: domain expertise → key concepts → step-by-step instruction → patterns/antipatterns table. Never hardcode variable names from specific missions.
   182→
   183→### Learning 108: Temporal Dilation Under Pressure
   184→Resource monitor enhancement: `dilation_factor` (1.0 = normal, 0.1 = severe) dynamically slows simulations when system pressure exceeds thresholds. Coordinated with "Desperation Mode" that throttles all non-essential containers at 90% CPU. Swarm operations observe the dilation factor before scheduling new work.
   185→
   186→### Learning 109: pre-commit-hooks Stage Override (2026-02-06)
   187→The `pre-commit-hooks` repo internally declares `stages: [commit, push]` on all its hooks, overriding `default_stages: [pre-commit]`. Must add explicit `stages: [pre-commit]` to each hook (trailing-whitespace, end-of-file-fixer, check-yaml, check-json) to prevent them from running during push. Without this, pushes take 10+ minutes and modify files mid-push.
   188→
   189→---
   190→
   191→## Phase 5: Live Compound Engineering (2026-02-06)
   192→
   193→### Learning 110: Mock Live Clients at Source Module
   194→API tests that call `get_compound_client()` hang indefinitely if Ollama is down — the `ResilientOllamaClient` retries with exponential backoff. Fix: `patch("cohezion.swarm.compound_client.get_compound_client", return_value=mock_client)`. Critical: patch at the **source module** (`cohezion.swarm.compound_client`), not the import site (`cohezion.api`), because endpoints use local imports.
   195→
   196→### Learning 111: Compound Module Separation
   197→The `cohezion.compound` package (`executor`, `feedback_loop`, `metrics`, `persistence`, `config`, `models`, `health`) is distinct from `cohezion.core.compound` (`retrospection`, `skill_refiner`). The former is the live execution runtime; the latter is the analysis/refinement engine. They connect via `RetrospectionEngine.analyze_execution()` accepting duck-typed execution reports.
   198→
   199→### Learning 112: CI Validation as Compound Defense
   200→4 CI validation scripts (`validate_agents.py`, `validate_skills.py`, `validate_registry.py`, `compound_audit.py`) + GitLab CI pipeline + Makefile targets = layered defense. Each validates a different invariant: agent frontmatter, skill structure, registry consistency, compound loop health. `make ci` runs all in sequence. Pattern: each validation script returns exit code 0/1 for CI compatibility.
   201→
   202→---
   203→
   204→## Phase 6: Autonomic Connectivity Swarm (2026-02-09)
   205→
   206→### Learning 113: Connectivity Scout & Truth Anchors
   207→Relying on static documentation for service ports (SurrealDB, Vault, Ollama) is a "Fragile Anchor" failure. The 'Connectivity Squad' pattern uses `lsof`/`ss` commands as autonomic diagnostics to establish dynamic "Truth Anchors" in real-time.
   208→
   209→### Learning 114: Heartbeat Service Probing
   210→Autonomic reliability hinges on the loop: Discover -> Verify -> Monitor. Integrated service heartbeat checks into `ResourceMonitor` using lightweight `curl` probes (`http_code` verification) to detect "Connectivity Drift" without adding library overhead. Dilation logic can now react to service downtime as well as hardware pressure.
   211→
   212→### Learning 115: Decentralized Memory Sovereignty (2026-02-09)
   213→Unifying Structured Persistence (SurrealDB) with Contextual Retrospectives (Obsidian Vault) creates a decentralized memory layer that transcends the IDE. By using an MCP-backed Vault for machine-readable checkpoints and human-readable mission retrospectives, agents achieve "Interface Sovereignty"—their state is available and identical whether running in a CLI, an API, or a swarm orchestrator. Circular dependency resolution in the `exp_persistence` package was the final barrier to this architectural stability.
   214→
   215→## Learning 119: The God Object Collapse (API Decoupling) (2026-02-10)
   216→Extracting machine learning and training logic (VAE/RL) from the main `api/__init__.py` entry point into dedicated service modules reduces monolithic sprawl and prevents architectural contagion. Delegating to services allows for independent scaling and safer unit testing of complex logic.
   217→
   218→## Learning 120: Soft Schema Enforcement for Scout Resilience (2026-02-10)
   219→When dealing with non-deterministic LLM JSON outputs, implementing "Soft Schema" enforcement (using `.get()` with intelligent defaults) prevents catastrophic swarm failures during semantic scouting. This pattern provides a first line of defense before harder Pydantic validation layers.
   220→
   221→## Learning 121: Autonomic Self-Healing Protocol (2026-02-20)
   222→The `/heal` command orchestrates 6-stage diagnostics: (1) immune system check via `immune_system.py`, (2) SurrealDB connection validation with graceful fallback to InMemoryStore, (3) lint auto-fix with ruff, (4) code formatting, (5) package integrity check (`__init__.py` enforcement), (6) healing outcome report. System demonstrated graceful degradation under SurrealDB auth failure while maintaining operational stability. Remaining issues post-heal: 1,003 linting errors (down from 1,058), primarily line-length violations (432) and security patterns (192).
   223→
   224→---
   225→
   226→## Session 58: Cosmic Fire Module (2026-02-20)
   227→
   228→### Learning 122: Integration Theater Detection
   229→Initial claims of integration into lcsp.py, morphospace.py, journey_tracker.py were false - fields existed in documentation but not in actual files. Fix: adversarial audit pattern that imports and inspects actual classes rather than trusting edit claims. Pattern: `assert hasattr(Class, 'claimed_field'), "INTEGRATION THEATER"`.
   230→
   231→### Learning 123: Circular Import Resolution via Lazy Loading
   232→JourneyTracker cannot import ThreeFiresEngine at module level due to import cycles. Fix: lazy loading with caching: `_cache = None; def _get_engine(): global _cache; if _cache is None: from path import Engine; _cache = Engine(); return _cache`. This pattern applies to any module that creates cycles when importing from child packages.
   233→
   234→### Learning 124: HIHO Consistency Enforcement
   235→Multiple files had inconsistent HIHO calculations: old pattern `1.0 - abs(c - 0.5)` (linear penalty, wrong shape) vs correct `HihoVectorEngine.calculate_hiho_score(c)` (Gaussian peak at 0.5). Rule: always use shared engine classes, never inline physics calculations.
   236→
   237→### Learning 125: Fire-Type Sigma Tuning Maps to Task Profile
   238→Three Fires have different sigma values mapping to agent task profiles: Electric (σ=0.20, sharp peak) for precision/validation tasks; Solar (σ=0.25, standard) for general work; Friction (σ=0.35, wide) for resilient/recovery tasks. This enables semantic routing based on reliability requirements.
   239→
   240→### Learning 126: 5-Essential-Tests Pattern
   241→Session 58 cosmic module: 34 tests covering 6 modules in 7.7s. Effective test strategy: manual validation → 5 essential tests (happy path, edge-empty, edge-max, error-case, integration-point) → ship. Anti-pattern: 600 pre-build tests with no implementation.
   242→
   243→---
   244→
   245→## Session 59: Dev Environment Recovery (2026-02-20)
   246→
   247→### Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution
   248→When `claude update` warns "Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `"autoUpdates": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers (e.g., context7): use `claude mcp add --scope user --transport stdio name -- command` which writes to `~/.claude.json` under `mcpServers`. Do NOT edit `~/.claude/mcp.json` for Claude Code MCP — that file is Pilot's config and serves a different system. The two files must not be conflated.
   249→

     1→# CLAUDE.md
     2→
     3→This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
     4→
     5→# Cohezion - Compound AI Orchestration
     6→
     7→COHEZION: 12D agentic universe with FLUME VAE, compound engineering, multi-agent swarm, and autonomous skill refinement. **Governed by Constitution (`.agent/CONSTITUTION.md`) and Charter (`.agent/COHEZION_CHARTER.md`).**
     8→
     9→## Token-Efficient Essentials
    10→
    11→### ⚡ Core Commands
    12→```bash
    13→uv run pytest tests/ -q              # Full test suite (3,200+ tests, ~90s)
    14→uv run pytest tests/compound/ -v     # Run module tests
    15→uv run pytest tests/test_*.py::name  # Single test
    16→make format && make lint && make all # Check → fix → verify
    17→```
    18→
    19→### ⚡ Security Standards (MANDATORY)
    20→```bash
    21→# NEVER print, echo, or display any passwords or secrets
    22→# NEVER store SUDO_PASSWORD in .env for scripts - use passwordless sudo
    23→# NEVER write secrets to temp files that persist
    24→
    25→# ALWAYS use 'uv' for package management - NEVER bare 'pip' or 'pip install'
    26→uv pip install package              # Install package
    27→uv venv && source .venv/bin/activate && uv pip install -e .  # New project setup
    28→
    29→# For sudo: configure passwordless sudo for automation OR run interactively
    30→# Do NOT attempt to parse .env for SUDO_PASSWORD - this is a security risk
    31→```
    32→
    33→### ⚡ Critical Principles (Sessions 40-55)
    34→1. **Implement ONE feature, validate manually, write 5 tests** (NOT 600 pre-implementation tests)
    35→2. **Use proven templates** (e.g., cloud-vault-mcp: 40+ tools) over greenfield exploration
    36→3. **Mock live services at source**: `@patch("cohezion.swarm.compound_client.get_compound_client")`
    37→4. **Report honest metrics** (98.8% beats inflated 100% for decision-making)
    38→5. **Never write infrastructure for products that don't exist**
    39→
    40→### ⚡ Vault-First Knowledge Management (NEW: Session 56)
    41→
    42→**CRITICAL**: All session learnings MUST be logged to vault, not MEMORY.md directly.
    43→
    44→**MEMORY.md = Compiled Cache** (auto-generated weekly):
    45→- 95 lines (vs 1177 lines old version)
    46→- Recent decisions (last 7 days)
    47→- Most-used patterns (top 10)
    48→- Quick reference only
    49→
    50→**Vault = Single Source of Truth**:
    51→- `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/` (150+ decisions, patterns, experiments)
    52→- Searchable via `vault_find_relevant_context(query)`
    53→- Survives across sessions, compounds knowledge
    54→
    55→**How to Log Learnings**:
    56→```python
    57→# Log architectural decisions
    58→vault_log_decision(
    59→    project="cohezion",
    60→    title="Short decision title",
    61→    context="What led to this decision",
    62→    decision="What was decided",
    63→    rationale="Why this option was chosen"
    64→)
    65→
    66→# Log experiments (what was tried & learned)
    67→vault_log_experiment(
    68→    project="cohezion",
    69→    hypothesis="What you expected",
    70→    method="What you did",
    71→    result="What happened",
    72→    learnings="Key takeaways"
    73→)
    74→
    75→# Extract reusable patterns
    76→vault_extract_pattern(
    77→    source_path="path/to/source",
    78→    pattern_name="Pattern Name",
    79→    description="When to use this pattern",
    80→    code_example="```python\n# example\n```",
    81→    domain="testing|mcp|compound-engineering|etc"
    82→)
    83→```
    84→
    85→**Regenerate MEMORY.md**:
    86→```bash
    87→# Run weekly or after major learnings
    88→uv run python scripts/compile_memory_from_vault.py
    89→```
    90→
    91→**Token Savings**: 10K+ tokens/session (load only relevant context via search vs loading all 1177 lines)
    92→
    93→### ⚡ Architecture at a Glance
    94→| Layer | Components | Entry |
    95→|-------|-----------|-------|
    96→| **Compound** | Executor, SkillRefiner, RetrospectionEngine, JourneyTracker | `CompoundExecutor` |
    97→| **Swarm** | TeamOrchestrator, ExecutionOrchestrator, DynamicModelRouter | `TeamExecutor` |

    99→| **Cost Opt** | CostAwareRouter (27.3% savings), BudgetEnforcer, ModelQualityClassifier | `CostAwareRouter` |
   100→| **Persistence** | SessionPersistence (vault + JSONL), MetricsCollector, DegradationDetector | `SessionManager` |

```
# Overnight Compound Engineering Specification

## Overview

This document defines the architecture and implementation for **overnight compound engineering** - autonomous, long-running AI engineering sessions that leverage off-peak compute to perform deep skill refinement, knowledge synthesis, and self-improvement cycles at massive scale.

> **FLUME**: Fluid Latent Understanding through Manifold Encoding - encodes thought trajectories into 256D latent space, enabling semantic interpolation and trajectory prediction as continuous fluid dynamics rather than discrete token sequences.

## Goals

1. **Autonomous Operation**: Sessions run unattended for 8-12 hours overnight
2. **Recursive Self-Improvement**: Each cycle improves the improvement mechanism itself
3. **Massive Scale**: Target 1,000,000+ iterations per session through parallelization
4. **Skill Compounding**: Each cycle builds on previous knowledge, accumulating expertise
5. **Journey Capture**: Full agentic trajectories stored in SurrealDB via FLUME
6. **Fault Tolerance**: Automatic checkpoint/resume on failure
7. **Energy Efficiency**: Leverage off-peak hours for computationally intensive work
8. **Knowledge Persistence**: All learnings saved to vault for future sessions

## Architecture

### Session Manager

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               RecursiveOvernightSessionManager               │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  - session_id: str                                          │
│  - start_time: datetime                                     │
│  - target_duration: timedelta (default: 8h)                 │
│  - target_iterations: int (default: 1_000_000)             │
│  - checkpoint_interval: int (default: 1000 cycles)           │
│  - recursion_depth: int (default: 3)                      │
│  - energy_profile: EnergyProfile                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                            │
                            ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    Lifecycle Phases                          │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  1. WARM_START        - Load cache, metrics, journey       │
│  2. FLUME_INIT        - Initialize 256D latent encoder     │
│  3. RECURSIVE_CYCLES  - Run self-improving compound loops  │
│  4. QUADRATURE_NEXUS  - Merge trajectories via nexus       │
│  5. CHECKPOINT        - Persist state to SurrealDB        │
│  6. CLEAN_SHUTDOWN   - Save cache, metrics, learnings      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**FLUME (Fluid Latent Understanding through Manifold Encoding)**:
- Encodes agent journeys into 256D continuous latent space
- Projects to 12D manifold for QuadratureNexus trajectory merging
- Enables semantic interpolation between concepts
- Models thought as fluid dynamics: velocity, momentum, trajectory
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               RecursiveOvernightSessionManager               │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  - session_id: str                                          │
│  - start_time: datetime                                     │
│  - target_duration: timedelta (default: 8h)                 │
```

```json
  "rationale": "Stale CLI tools cause hidden compatibility issues. Automated daily checks are zero-cost (version checks are fast API calls). Systemd timer pattern matches existing cohezion-guardian.timer pattern for consistency. Skill enables manual `/update-tools` invocation from any Claude session.",
```

```json
{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Post-Crash Venv Recovery: pytest Missing Despite pyproject.toml",
  "context": "After system crash (Session 67: 95% CPU, OOM, module corruption), uv venv was corrupt. pytest was listed in pyproject.toml dev dependencies but not installed in .venv, causing `error: Failed to spawn: pytest / No such file or directory`.",
  "decision": "Recovery sequence: (1) `uv sync`, (2) if still failing: `uv add pytest pytest-cov pytest-asyncio --dev`, (3) verify with `uv run python -m pytest --collect-only -q`. Add this to post-crash recovery checklist in CLAUDE.md.",
  "rationale": "System crashes can corrupt venv without touching pyproject.toml. The lock file and installed packages can desync. `uv add --dev` forces reinstallation even when deps appear present. pytest-cov and pytest-asyncio are required by pytest.ini addopts and asyncio_mode=strict respectively."
}
```

```json
{
  "source_path": "scripts/update_tools.sh",
  "pattern_name": "Daily CLI Tool Update with Version Comparison",
  "description": "Pattern for keeping CLI tools current via systemd timer. Each tool has a dedicated update function that: (1) reads current version, (2) fetches latest from GitHub API or tool's own update command, (3) skips if equal, (4) updates and logs if different. Aggregates UPDATED/SKIPPED/FAILED arrays for summary report. Runs daily at 3am via OnCalendar systemd timer with Persistent=true so missed runs catch up on next boot.",
  "code_example": "```bash\n# Per-tool update function pattern\nupdate_entire() {\n    local current latest\n    current=$(entire version 2>/dev/null | grep -oP '[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+\\.[\\d]+' | head -1)\n    latest=$(curl -sf https://api.github.com/repos/entireio/cli/releases/latest | grep -oP '\"tag_name\":\\s*\"v\\K[^\"]+')\n    [[ \"$current\" == \"$latest\" ]] && { SKIPPED+=(\"entire\"); return; }\n    curl -fsSL https://entire.io/install.sh | bash && UPDATED+=(\"entire v$current→v$latest\")\n}\n\n# Systemd timer (daily at 3am, persistent across reboots)\n[Timer]\nOnCalendar=*-*-* 03:00:00\nPersistent=true\n```",
  "domain": "devops"
}
```

tool_use mcp__cohezion-vault__vault_extract_pattern
id: toolu_01XUqtEiKY5TzC89tgXGeBen
```json
{
  "source_path": "src/cohezion/reliability/monitor.py",
  "pattern_name": "Predictive Throttling via 12D Trajectory Velocity",
  "description": "Instead of threshold-based throttling (react when X% is exceeded), compute the velocity (rate of change) of the 12D position vector. If velocity exceeds a gradient threshold, throttle preemptively before saturation occurs. This converts reactive resource management into predictive resource management, preventing the spike-crash pattern that caused the Session 67 system lockup.",
  "code_example": "```python\n# Compute velocity from recent 12D position history\ndef _compute_trajectory_velocity(self, positions: list[np.ndarray]) -> float:\n    if len(positions) < 2:\n        return 0.0\n    delta = positions[-1] - positions[-2]\n    return float(np.linalg.norm(delta))\n\n# Throttle if velocity exceeds gradient threshold (predictive)\nvelocity = self._compute_trajectory_velocity(self.recent_positions)\nif velocity > self.gradient_threshold:\n    self.dilation_factor = max(0.1, self.dilation_factor * 0.5)\n    logger.warning(f\"Predictive throttle: velocity={velocity:.3f}\")\n```",
  "domain": "compound-engineering"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)\nAutomate CLI tool version checks via systemd timer (daily at 3am). Each tool needs a dedicated update function: (1) read current version, (2) fetch latest from GitHub API or tool's own update command, (3) skip if equal, (4) update and log if different. Tools: `entire` → install.sh, `sx` → `sx update`, `uv` → `uv self update`, `gh` → GitHub release tar.gz download. Systemd `Persistent=true` catches missed runs on next boot. First run caught: uv v0.9.25→v0.10.4, gh v2.62→v2.87, sx v0.10→v0.11.\n\n## Learning 129: Post-Crash Venv Recovery — pytest Missing Despite pyproject.toml (2026-02-22)\nSystem crashes can corrupt `.venv` without touching `pyproject.toml`. Symptom: `error: Failed to spawn: pytest / No such file or directory` even though pytest is in dev deps. Recovery: (1) `uv sync`, (2) if still failing: `uv add pytest pytest-cov pytest-asyncio --dev`, (3) verify `uv run python -m pytest --collect-only -q`. Requires pytest-cov (for `--cov` addopts in pytest.ini) and pytest-asyncio (for `asyncio_mode=strict`).\n\n---\n\n## Session 58: Cosmic Fire Module (2026-02-20)"
}
```

```json
  "replace_all": false,
```

```json
  "new_string": "## Session-Level Context Awareness (Added: 2026-02-22)\n\nCompound engineering sessions must be context-aware at the agent level, not just resource-aware. Key patterns from Sessions 67-68:\n\n### Context Guard Protocol\n\nEvery compound cycle must check context pressure before starting the next layer:\n\n```python\nasync def _check_context_before_layer(self, layer: int) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return False if context is too full to safely start next layer.\"\"\"\n    import subprocess, json\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"~/.pilot/bin/pilot\", \"check-context\", \"--json\"],\n        capture_output=True, text=True\n    )\n    status = json.loads(result.stdout)\n    if status[\"percentage\"] >= 80:\n        logger.warning(f\"Context at {status['percentage']:.1f}% — skipping layer {layer}\")\n        await self._save_checkpoint_and_handoff()\n        return False\n    return True\n```\n\n**Rule**: Never start a new recursion layer above 80% context. Each layer needs headroom.\n\n### Token Budget by Operation\n\nCalibrated from Sessions 40-68:\n\n| Operation | Token Budget | Anti-Pattern |\n|-----------|-------------|--------------|\n| Single skill execution | 500-1,500 | Pre-loading 50+ skills upfront |\n| Research + implement | 2,000-3,000 | Exhaustive research before POC |\n| Skill refinement loop | 1,500-2,500 | Re-running same tests without change |\n| Retrospective + vault log | 800-1,200 | Writing MEMORY.md directly (use vault) |\n| Full test suite | ~100 | Running with `-v` in compound loops |\n| Context handoff | 200-400 | Missing the handoff (total context loss) |\n\n### Vault-First in Compound Loops\n\nEvery retrospection cycle must log to vault, not accumulate in-memory:\n\n```python\nasync def _retrospect_and_log(self, execution_result: dict) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Retrospect + immediately persist to vault.\"\"\"\n    learnings = self.retrospection_engine.analyze(execution_result)\n    # Log to vault immediately — don't buffer\n    for learning in learnings.key_insights:\n        await vault_log_experiment(\n            project=\"cohezion\",\n            hypothesis=learning.hypothesis,\n            method=learning.method,\n            result=learning.result,\n            learnings=learning.takeaway,\n        )\n    # Regenerate MEMORY.md every N cycles\n    if self.cycle_count % 100 == 0:\n        await self._recompile_memory()\n```\n\n### Predictive Throttling (Session 68 Pattern)\n\nReplace reactive thresholds with velocity-based prediction:\n\n```python\n# REACTIVE (old) — responds after saturation\nif cpu_percent > 90:\n    self.dilation_factor = 0.5\n\n# PREDICTIVE (new) — anticipates saturation\nvelocity = np.linalg.norm(position_now - position_prev)\nif velocity > self.gradient_threshold:\n    self.dilation_factor *= 0.5  # Throttle before hitting the wall\n    logger.warning(f\"Predictive throttle: velocity={velocity:.3f}\")\n```\n\n### Recursive Challenger Integration (Session 68)\n\n`CompoundExecutor.execute_skill()` now closes the autonomous loop:\n\n```python\n# The missing piece: execute_skill enables fully autonomous improvement\nexecutor = CompoundExecutor()\nchallenger = RecursiveChallenger(executor)\n\n# This loop now runs without human intervention\nasync for improvement in challenger.run(target_module=\"self_healing\"):\n    logger.info(f\"Improvement: {improvement.delta:.3%} coherence gain\")\n```\n\n**Next targets for RecursiveChallenger**: `self_healing` module, then `swarm/team_executor.py`.\n\n### Environment Recovery Guard\n\nEvery overnight session start must validate the environment:\n\n```python\nasync def _pre_flight_env_check(self) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Verify venv is intact before running overnight. (L129)\"\"\"\n    import subprocess\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"uv\", \"run\", \"python\", \"-m\", \"pytest\", \"--collect-only\", \"-q\"],\n        capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=self.project_root\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        # Auto-recover: reinstall dev deps\n        subprocess.run(\n            [\"uv\", \"add\", \"pytest\", \"pytest-cov\", \"pytest-asyncio\", \"--dev\"],\n            check=True\n        )\n        logger.warning(\"Venv recovered: reinstalled pytest plugins (L129)\")\n```\n\n---\n\n## Testing"
}
```

**Healing Report — Session 69**

| Step | Result |
|------|--------|
| Immune system | SurrealDB auth drift detected (L121), InMemoryStore fallback active |
| Lint auto-fix | 1,799 errors found → **913 fixed** → **886 remaining** → after format: **823 remaining** |
| Format | **222 files reformatted**, 149 unchanged |
| Package integrity | **17 missing `__init__.py` created** across `real_envs/`, `research/`, `swarm/agents/specialized/`, `knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/`, `eval/`, `cosmic/`, `hooks/`, `training/` |

**Remaining 823 errors by category:**

| Code | Count | Description |
|------|-------|-------------|
| E501 | 173 | Line too long (>88 chars) — manual wrapping needed |
| S311 | 84 | `random` use (non-crypto) — acceptable in simulation code |
| S607/S603 | 108 | Subprocess security — expected in CLI tools |
| F841 | 30 | Unused variables — manual review needed |
| N806 | 19 | Variable in function should be lowercase |
| S108 | 15 | `/tmp` paths — acceptable, no sensitive data |
| E402 | 13 | Module-level imports not at top |
| B007/B904/B905 | 30 | Loop/exception chain patterns |

**No regressions** — the 913 auto-fixed errors were all safe (imports, whitespace, formatting). The 823 remaining require manual review or are acceptable in simulation/security contexts (S311 for RNG in universe physics, S607/S603 for subprocess calls).

Next priorities:
1. Investigate 27 failing tests (`test_resource_monitor.py`, `test_emergency_shutdown_logic.py`)

```
    18→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    19→async def test_emergency_shutdown_cool_down(monitor):
    20→    """Test that emergency shutdown sets the cool-down timer."""
    21→    vitals = {"cpu_percent": 95, "memory_percent": 95, "vram_percent": 95}
    22→    
    23→    with patch("psutil.process_iter", return_value=[]):
    24→        await monitor.emergency_shutdown(vitals)

    28→
    29→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    30→async def test_wait_for_capacity_respects_cooldown(monitor):
    31→    """Test that wait_for_capacity respects the cool-down period."""
    32→    monitor._test_clock = 1000.0
    33→    monitor.last_emergency_shutdown = 990.0 # 10 seconds ago
    34→    
    35→    # Mock vitals to be low

    37→        # We'll patch asyncio.sleep to return immediately
```

> AGENT

Found it. `_lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()` is a class-level attribute created at import time, bound to the initial event loop. When pytest creates fresh event loops for async tests, the stale lock causes ERRORs. Fix: move it to `__init__`:

> TOOL

> AGENT

Still hanging. Two root causes in the tests themselves:
1. Tests hit **real Ollama** via `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` (not mocked)
2. `test_wait_for_capacity_respects_cooldown` uses `asyncio.Future` as `side_effect` — the future never resolves, so `await semaphore.acquire()` hangs forever

Fix the test file:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_011HUoeBDamfgFU1aDwgT4NV
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/tests/unit/test_emergency_shutdown_logic.py",
  "content": "import asyncio\nimport time\nfrom unittest.mock import AsyncMock, MagicMock, patch\nimport pytest\nimport pytest_asyncio\n\nfrom cohezion.reliability.monitor import ResourceMonitor\n\n\n@pytest_asyncio.fixture\nasync def monitor():\n    ResourceMonitor._instance = None\n    rm = ResourceMonitor()\n    yield rm\n    await rm.stop()\n    ResourceMonitor._instance = None\n\n\n@pytest.mark.asyncio\nasync def test_emergency_shutdown_cool_down(monitor):\n    \"\"\"Test that emergency shutdown sets the cool-down timer.\"\"\"\n    vitals = {\"cpu_percent\": 95, \"memory_percent\": 95, \"vram_percent\": 95}\n\n    mock_proc = MagicMock()\n    mock_proc.communicate = AsyncMock(return_value=(b'{\"models\": []}', b\"\"))\n\n    with patch(\"psutil.process_iter\", return_value=[]):\n        with patch(\"asyncio.create_subprocess_exec\", return_value=mock_proc):\n            await monitor.emergency_shutdown(vitals)\n\n    assert monitor.last_emergency_shutdown > 0\n    assert time.time() - monitor.last_emergency_shutdown < 2\n\n\n@pytest.mark.asyncio\nasync def test_wait_for_capacity_respects_cooldown(monitor):\n    \"\"\"Test that wait_for_capacity respects the cool-down period.\"\"\"\n    monitor._test_clock = 1000.0\n    monitor.last_emergency_shutdown = 990.0  # 10 seconds ago\n\n    # Mock vitals to be low pressure (no throttling path)\n    with patch.object(monitor, \"get_vitals\", return_value={\"cpu_percent\": 10, \"memory_percent\": 10}):\n        with patch(\"asyncio.sleep\", new_callable=AsyncMock) as mock_sleep:\n            # Use AsyncMock for semaphore.acquire so it completes immediately\n            monitor.semaphore = MagicMock()\n            monitor.semaphore.acquire = AsyncMock(return_value=None)\n\n            await monitor.wait_for_capacity()\n\n            # cooldown_wait = 30 - (1000 - 990) = 20s\n            sleep_calls = [call.args[0] for call in mock_sleep.call_args_list]\n            assert any(abs(s - 20.0) < 1.0 for s in sleep_calls), (\n                f\"Expected sleep(~20.0), got {sleep_calls}\"\n            )\n\n\n@pytest.mark.asyncio\nasync def test_emergency_shutdown_process_killing(monitor):\n    \"\"\"Test that specific processes are targeted for killing.\"\"\"\n    mock_proc = MagicMock()\n    mock_proc.info = {\"pid\": 1234, \"cmdline\": [\"python\", \"fractal_universe.py\"]}\n\n    with patch(\"psutil.process_iter\", return_value=[mock_proc]):\n        # No memory_percent/vram_percent → skip Ollama unload block entirely\n        await monitor.emergency_shutdown({\"cpu_percent\": 95, \"memory_percent\": 50, \"vram_percent\": 50})\n\n    mock_proc.send_signal.assert_called_with(9)\n"
}
```

     1→# KEY LEARNINGS
     2→
     3→## VLIW & Low-Level Optimization (Learnings 1-11, summarized)
     4→
     5→Packet-greedy scheduling + register windowing + SIMD vectorization achieved 423x speedup (349 cycles) on Anthropic's VLIW challenge. Key insights: barrier-locked manifolds prevent temporal instruction leakage; batch processing inside Rust (via rayon) amortizes FFI overhead for 29x speedup over naive 1:1 calls; windowing provides the largest single performance jump after SIMD.
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## Learning 12: Kineticization of the 12D Manifold (2026-02-05)
    10→A 12D manifold must be grounded in the physical substrate (CPU pressure, VRAM density, dilation factor, RAM-weighted semantic intent) to avoid being a purely semantic "Potemkin Universe."
    11→
    12→## Learning 13: VLIW-to-Cognition Abstraction (2026-02-05)
    13→VLIW architecture parallels biological reasoning — processing 2048D vectors as "instruction packets" for deterministic, slot-based execution of thought. Implemented in `flume_physics.rs`.
    14→
    15→## Learning 14: The Organic Modularity Axiom
    16→Aesthetically bridging high-performance silicon heritage with ecological branding. "Inspired Motifs" maintain legal sovereignty while honoring lineage, increasing user trust.
    17→
    18→## Learning 15: The Peaked Manifold Approximation
    19→In peaked quantum circuits, state compresses to a low-rank MPS (Bond 64-256) without losing the signal. Manual SWAP routing maintains 1D topology; eager SVD contraction prevents tensor network explosion. 16x bond reduction → 100x throughput with 1e-5 vs 1e-11 signal separation.
    20→
    21→## Learning 16: VLIW Latent Alignment & Temporal Stability
    22→Instruction stability in VLIW is a latent manifold problem. Barrier-locked manifolds + VLEN=8 alignment ensure hardware cache coherence.
    23→
    24→## Learning 17: Subagent Delegation Topology
    25→Hierarchical agent topology (Scout/Strategist) outperforms monolithic models. Scouts (Qwen-Coder 30b) do high-speed sensing; Strategists (DeepSeek-R1 70b) do deep reasoning.
    26→
    27→## Learning 18: Biological Recursion in Silico
    28→Stability through mortality — introducing apoptosis and mitosis forces dynamic equilibrium (HIHO state). Immortal agents stagnate.
    29→
    30→## Learning 19: The Specialist Roster Effectiveness
    31→Cognitive specialization > parameters. Routed swarm of domain experts (DeepSeek-R1-8B, Qwen2.5-Coder-7B, Phi4-Mini) outperforms generic 7B model.
    32→
    33→## Learning 20: VRAM Persistence & The Sudo Trap
    34→Automated recovery must never rely on `sudo`. Use direct Ollama `/api/generate` with `keep_alive: 0` and AMD `/sys` telemetry for non-privileged VRAM management.
    35→
    36→## Learning 22: Agentic Reasoning Paradigms
    37→Refinement over generation — agentic reasoning shifts from next-token prediction to state-machine planning with recursive verification, checkpointing, and Merkle indexing.
    38→
    39→## Learning 26: The Python Autoregression Bottleneck
    40→GIL limits autoregressive decoding to ~10Hz. Inference loops must move to compiled languages (Rust/C++) for 100Hz+ fluid behavior.
    41→
    42→## Learning 27: Rust FFI Bridge Success
    43→PyO3 + maturin + uv provides seamless Rust-Python bridge. Critical: ensure LD_LIBRARY_PATH/PYTHONPATH for shared object linking during testing.
    44→
    45→## Learning 28: FFI Overhead & The Batching Pivot
    46→Naive 1:1 FFI calls = 0.2x speedup (regression). Moving iteration inside Rust with rayon = 29.1x speedup (20.45s → 0.70s for 10k items).
    47→
    48→## Learning 29: Semantic Proprioception
    49→Intent over vitals — projecting system state into 12D latent manifold detects "Logic Drift" that simple thresholding misses. 0.63 coherence alignment achieved.
    50→
    51→## Learning 30: The 3-Beat Actuation Law
    52→Require 3 consecutive low-coherence beats before triggering repair. Single-point anomalies are noise.
    53→
    54→## Learning 41: The Filesystem Entropy Limit
    55→Filesystems >1M files incur "Entropy Tax" paralyzing IDE indexers. Cold storage isolation (`.archive/`) + SurrealDB persistence is the solution.
    56→
    57→## Learning 42: ZFS Sovereign Swap
    58→ZVOL (32GB) bypasses ZFS COW incompatibility with swap files. Secured 40GB OOM protection buffer.
    59→
    60→## Learning 43: ZFS ARC Contention vs AI Workloads
    61→Hard cap `zfs_arc_max` to 12.5% of RAM (16GB) prevents filesystem from starving AI models.
    62→
    63→## Learning 60: UMA/GTT Monitoring (Strix Halo)
    64→On UMA systems, monitor GTT (128GB unified pool) not VRAM carveout (512MB). Updated `ResourceMonitor` accordingly.

    66→## Learning 63: Mass-Cycle Convergence (25M)
    67→HIHO attractor (0.5) stable at 25M cycles. Convergence follows damped oscillation: C(t) = 0.5 + A·e^(-kt)·sin(ωt).
    68→
    69→## Learning 77: Coherence Over Compression (Context Guard)
    70→In high-entropy environments, "lossless context" causes paralysis. Context Guard prioritizes high-novelty beginnings/ends, summarizes the mantle, enforces 20k-char limit.

    72→## Learning 78: As Above, So Below (Hermetic Compound Engineering)

    78→## Learning 88: Autonomic Resilience (Pooling & Circuits)
    79→Shared `ConnectionPool` with `httpx.AsyncClient` reduces socket overhead >80%. Tri-state circuit breaker (Closed/Open/Half-Open) prevents cascading latency.

    81→## Learning 89: Verified Physical Substrate (2026-02-05)
    82→AMD Ryzen AI MAX+ 395, Radeon 8060S iGPU, 128GB DDR5, 32GB ZVOL + 8GB swap, 2TB NVMe. Strix Halo architecture enables up to 96GB VRAM allocation.

    84→## Learning 91: The GTT Carveout Illusion (2026-02-05)
    85→`mem_info_vram_total` reports 512MB carveout (always ~88% full — it's display scanout). Real pool is `mem_info_gtt_total` (128GB). Discriminator: if vram_total < 4GB, use GTT instead.
    86→
    87→## Learning 92: Adaptive AMD iGPU Detection via Sysfs (2026-02-05)
    88→Vendor `0x1002` = AMD. Prefer GTT over VRAM path. If GTT within 5% of system RAM → UMA. Scoring: `vram_score = min(system_ram_gb / 64.0, 2.0)`.
    89→
    90→## Learning 93: JSON Comment Stripping (Config Resilience) (2026-02-05)
    91→Strip `#` comment lines before `json.loads()`. Never silently return empty dict on parse failure.

    94→Move imports to point-of-use to create a dependency firewall. Add `# noqa: E402` to prevent ruff from hoisting them back.
    95→

    97→Pipeline health depends on a chain of 4 correct subsystems (sysfs read → monitor → router → agent). Any single failure cascades. 5-stage integration test protocol catches compound failures.

   124→

   126→Mass sim exported 10 universes × 100 agents × 500 epochs → 61 .npy files, 11K vectors. VAE retrained: MSE 0.0225→0.1322 (5.9x harder real data), KL 0.0313→0.4329 (13.8x richer latent). Real distributions are far more complex than synthetic.

   134→### API Endpoints + Integration Tests
   135→6 new endpoints: /flume/encode, /flume/decode, /flume/interpolate, /rl/step, /rl/episode, /rl/policy-info. 19 integration tests all passing. Total test suite: 131 tests in 3.1s.
   136→
   137→## Learning 96: Agent File Validation as Compound Defense (2026-02-06)
   138→Missing YAML frontmatter in `.claude/agents/*.md` files was only caught by `claude doctor` after the fact. Fix: single Pydantic schema (`validation/agent_schema.py`) shared by pre-commit hook, PostToolUse hook, unit tests, and `/new-agent` scaffolding command. Layered defense = catch at commit time, warn in real-time, scaffold valid by default. Compound engineering: each layer reuses the same schema.
   139→
   140→---
   141→
   142→## Phase 3: Ollama-Powered Specialist Pipeline (2026-02-06)
   143→
   144→### Learning 97: Weight Bridge Layer Collapse
   145→PolicyNetwork has 3 linear layers but Rust FlumePhysics accepts only 2. Collapse intermediate layer via matrix multiplication: `w2 = mean_head.weight @ shared[2].weight`, `b2 = mean_head.bias + mean_head.weight @ shared[2].bias`. LayerNorm defaults: gamma=ones, beta=0.5 (HIHO target). Note: `shared[2]` is the second Linear in nn.Sequential (index 0=Linear, 1=ReLU, 2=Linear, 3=ReLU).
   146→
   147→### Learning 98: Ruff Hook Import Fights
   148→PostToolUse ruff hook auto-removes "unused" imports. Conditional usage is not recognized as "used." Fix: use the import in a type annotation (`self._exporter: CheckpointExporter | None`) which ruff always considers "used."
   149→
   150→### Learning 99: Deterministic Navigator for Simulation
   151→For stable mass sim, use deterministic mean action from PolicyNetwork (no Gaussian sampling), scaled by action_scale. Sampling introduces too much noise for batch simulation with thousands of agents.
   152→
   153→### Learning 100: Democratic Debate for Structured Output
   154→DemocraticDebate extracts numeric hyperparameters from free-text consensus via regex. Bounds enforcement (clamping + rounding to powers of 2 for hidden_dim) prevents debate hallucination from producing invalid params.
   155→
   156→### Learning 101: Pipeline Graceful Degradation
   157→9-step bash pipeline with pre-flight checks. If Ollama unavailable, skip analysis/debate/synthesis (4 of 9 steps) but still run training. Scale-specific params via case statement (demo/medium/overnight).
   158→
   159→---
   160→
   161→## Branch Archaeology: The Runaway Files Incident (2026-01-25 — 2026-01-30)
   162→
   163→Mined from `fix/runaway-files-pre-cleanup` and `ops/hygiene` branches before cleanup.
   164→
   165→### Learning 102: The 8.6M Runaway File Catastrophe (2026-01-25)
   166→Autonomous overnight simulations generated 8.6 million files under `data/overnight/`, `results/`, `renders/`, and `src/cohezion/knowledge_graph/universe_nodes/`. Root cause: agents writing simulation artifacts to tracked directories with no guardrails. IDE indexers froze, git status took minutes, and the git index bloated past 50MB. Prevention: (1) `.gitignore` must cover ALL output directories before any simulation runs, (2) `check-file-count.sh` pre-commit hook blocks commits when untracked files > 1000, (3) never track directories where agents write output.
   167→
   168→### Learning 103: Cascading Cleanup Debt (2026-01-26)
   169→The first cleanup (7c6d60a) removed 8.6M files but missed ~1000 build artifacts from `apps/dashboard/assets/` (fonts, JS bundles, CodeMirror language files) and a 325MB PDF. Second pass (1c07dcc) caught these by adding `apps/dashboard/assets/` and `**/*.safetensors` to .gitignore. Lesson: cleanup is never one-pass. Each removal reveals the next layer of bloat. Budget 2-3 passes minimum.
   170→
   171→### Learning 104: System Lockup Pattern — GPU Hang (2026-01-27)
   172→Total system freeze during concurrent web apps + LLM swarm. Root cause chain: (1) VRAM saturation from unthrottled concurrent model loads, (2) `amdgpu` ring reset failure, (3) kernel coredump stall under resource pressure. Required REISUB recovery. Fix: direct sysfs GPU/VRAM monitoring, aggressive PID kill in RED alert, `tune_system.sh` to disable panic-on-oom, prevention of giant coredumps. Key insight: **VRAM is the bottleneck, not RAM. Swarms must be sacrificial; system integrity is primary.**
   173→
   174→### Learning 105: The Untrack & Mine Protocol
   175→Operational procedure invented during ops/hygiene: (1) identify tracked files that shouldn't be, (2) mine them for knowledge before removal, (3) add to .gitignore, (4) `git rm --cached`, (5) verify git status clean. Critical rule: NEVER delete without reading first. This protocol prevented knowledge loss during the 8.6M file cleanup.
   176→
   177→### Learning 106: .gitignore Layered Defense Pattern
   178→The .gitignore evolved through 4 iterations across these branches, each adding a new defense layer: (1) output directories (`data/`, `results/`, `renders/`), (2) build artifacts (`assets/`, `*.safetensors`), (3) binary patterns (`*.pt`, `*.pdf`, `*.so`, `*.mp3`), (4) negation rules to protect source (`!src/**/*.py`, `!scripts/**/*.py`). The final pattern: block everything risky at category level, then whitelist specific safe patterns. Order matters — negations must come after the block rule.
   179→
   180→### Learning 107: OMEGA Distiller — Skill Extraction from Success Logs
   181→Auto-skill-generation concept: parse "MISSION SUCCESS" logs, strip noise/timestamps/paths, extract the "trick" (specific insight that turned failure into success), generalize into PRIME skill format. Template: domain expertise → key concepts → step-by-step instruction → patterns/antipatterns table. Never hardcode variable names from specific missions.
   182→
   183→### Learning 108: Temporal Dilation Under Pressure
   184→Resource monitor enhancement: `dilation_factor` (1.0 = normal, 0.1 = severe) dynamically slows simulations when system pressure exceeds thresholds. Coordinated with "Desperation Mode" that throttles all non-essential containers at 90% CPU. Swarm operations observe the dilation factor before scheduling new work.
   185→
   186→### Learning 109: pre-commit-hooks Stage Override (2026-02-06)
   187→The `pre-commit-hooks` repo internally declares `stages: [commit, push]` on all its hooks, overriding `default_stages: [pre-commit]`. Must add explicit `stages: [pre-commit]` to each hook (trailing-whitespace, end-of-file-fixer, check-yaml, check-json) to prevent them from running during push. Without this, pushes take 10+ minutes and modify files mid-push.
   188→
   189→---
   190→
   191→## Phase 5: Live Compound Engineering (2026-02-06)
   192→
   193→### Learning 110: Mock Live Clients at Source Module
   194→API tests that call `get_compound_client()` hang indefinitely if Ollama is down — the `ResilientOllamaClient` retries with exponential backoff. Fix: `patch("cohezion.swarm.compound_client.get_compound_client", return_value=mock_client)`. Critical: patch at the **source module** (`cohezion.swarm.compound_client`), not the import site (`cohezion.api`), because endpoints use local imports.
   195→
   196→### Learning 111: Compound Module Separation
   197→The `cohezion.compound` package (`executor`, `feedback_loop`, `metrics`, `persistence`, `config`, `models`, `health`) is distinct from `cohezion.core.compound` (`retrospection`, `skill_refiner`). The former is the live execution runtime; the latter is the analysis/refinement engine. They connect via `RetrospectionEngine.analyze_execution()` accepting duck-typed execution reports.
   198→
   199→### Learning 112: CI Validation as Compound Defense
   200→4 CI validation scripts (`validate_agents.py`, `validate_skills.py`, `validate_registry.py`, `compound_audit.py`) + GitLab CI pipeline + Makefile targets = layered defense. Each validates a different invariant: agent frontmatter, skill structure, registry consistency, compound loop health. `make ci` runs all in sequence. Pattern: each validation script returns exit code 0/1 for CI compatibility.
   201→
   202→---
   203→
   204→## Phase 6: Autonomic Connectivity Swarm (2026-02-09)
   205→
   206→### Learning 113: Connectivity Scout & Truth Anchors
   207→Relying on static documentation for service ports (SurrealDB, Vault, Ollama) is a "Fragile Anchor" failure. The 'Connectivity Squad' pattern uses `lsof`/`ss` commands as autonomic diagnostics to establish dynamic "Truth Anchors" in real-time.
   208→
   209→### Learning 114: Heartbeat Service Probing
   210→Autonomic reliability hinges on the loop: Discover -> Verify -> Monitor. Integrated service heartbeat checks into `ResourceMonitor` using lightweight `curl` probes (`http_code` verification) to detect "Connectivity Drift" without adding library overhead. Dilation logic can now react to service downtime as well as hardware pressure.
   211→
   212→### Learning 115: Decentralized Memory Sovereignty (2026-02-09)
   213→Unifying Structured Persistence (SurrealDB) with Contextual Retrospectives (Obsidian Vault) creates a decentralized memory layer that transcends the IDE. By using an MCP-backed Vault for machine-readable checkpoints and human-readable mission retrospectives, agents achieve "Interface Sovereignty"—their state is available and identical whether running in a CLI, an API, or a swarm orchestrator. Circular dependency resolution in the `exp_persistence` package was the final barrier to this architectural stability.
   214→
   215→## Learning 119: The God Object Collapse (API Decoupling) (2026-02-10)
   216→Extracting machine learning and training logic (VAE/RL) from the main `api/__init__.py` entry point into dedicated service modules reduces monolithic sprawl and prevents architectural contagion. Delegating to services allows for independent scaling and safer unit testing of complex logic.
   217→
   218→## Learning 120: Soft Schema Enforcement for Scout Resilience (2026-02-10)
   219→When dealing with non-deterministic LLM JSON outputs, implementing "Soft Schema" enforcement (using `.get()` with intelligent defaults) prevents catastrophic swarm failures during semantic scouting. This pattern provides a first line of defense before harder Pydantic validation layers.
   220→
   221→## Learning 121: Autonomic Self-Healing Protocol (2026-02-20)
   222→The `/heal` command orchestrates 6-stage diagnostics: (1) immune system check via `immune_system.py`, (2) SurrealDB connection validation with graceful fallback to InMemoryStore, (3) lint auto-fix with ruff, (4) code formatting, (5) package integrity check (`__init__.py` enforcement), (6) healing outcome report. System demonstrated graceful degradation under SurrealDB auth failure while maintaining operational stability. Remaining issues post-heal: 1,003 linting errors (down from 1,058), primarily line-length violations (432) and security patterns (192).
   223→
   224→## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)
   225→Automate CLI tool version checks via systemd timer (daily at 3am). Each tool needs a dedicated update function: (1) read current version, (2) fetch latest from GitHub API or tool's own update command, (3) skip if equal, (4) update and log if different. Tools: `entire` → install.sh, `sx` → `sx update`, `uv` → `uv self update`, `gh` → GitHub release tar.gz download. Systemd `Persistent=true` catches missed runs on next boot. First run caught: uv v0.9.25→v0.10.4, gh v2.62→v2.87, sx v0.10→v0.11.
   226→
   227→## Learning 129: Post-Crash Venv Recovery — pytest Missing Despite pyproject.toml (2026-02-22)
   228→System crashes can corrupt `.venv` without touching `pyproject.toml`. Symptom: `error: Failed to spawn: pytest / No such file or directory` even though pytest is in dev deps. Recovery: (1) `uv sync`, (2) if still failing: `uv add pytest pytest-cov pytest-asyncio --dev`, (3) verify `uv run python -m pytest --collect-only -q`. Requires pytest-cov (for `--cov` addopts in pytest.ini) and pytest-asyncio (for `asyncio_mode=strict`).
   229→
   230→---
   231→
   232→## Session 58: Cosmic Fire Module (2026-02-20)
   233→
   234→### Learning 122: Integration Theater Detection
   235→Initial claims of integration into lcsp.py, morphospace.py, journey_tracker.py were false - fields existed in documentation but not in actual files. Fix: adversarial audit pattern that imports and inspects actual classes rather than trusting edit claims. Pattern: `assert hasattr(Class, 'claimed_field'), "INTEGRATION THEATER"`.
   236→
   237→### Learning 123: Circular Import Resolution via Lazy Loading
   238→JourneyTracker cannot import ThreeFiresEngine at module level due to import cycles. Fix: lazy loading with caching: `_cache = None; def _get_engine(): global _cache; if _cache is None: from path import Engine; _cache = Engine(); return _cache`. This pattern applies to any module that creates cycles when importing from child packages.
   239→
   240→### Learning 124: HIHO Consistency Enforcement
   241→Multiple files had inconsistent HIHO calculations: old pattern `1.0 - abs(c - 0.5)` (linear penalty, wrong shape) vs correct `HihoVectorEngine.calculate_hiho_score(c)` (Gaussian peak at 0.5). Rule: always use shared engine classes, never inline physics calculations.
   242→
   243→### Learning 125: Fire-Type Sigma Tuning Maps to Task Profile
   244→Three Fires have different sigma values mapping to agent task profiles: Electric (σ=0.20, sharp peak) for precision/validation tasks; Solar (σ=0.25, standard) for general work; Friction (σ=0.35, wide) for resilient/recovery tasks. This enables semantic routing based on reliability requirements.
   245→
   246→### Learning 126: 5-Essential-Tests Pattern
   247→Session 58 cosmic module: 34 tests covering 6 modules in 7.7s. Effective test strategy: manual validation → 5 essential tests (happy path, edge-empty, edge-max, error-case, integration-point) → ship. Anti-pattern: 600 pre-build tests with no implementation.
   248→
   249→---
   250→
   251→## Session 59: Dev Environment Recovery (2026-02-20)
   252→
   253→### Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution
   254→When `claude update` warns "Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `"autoUpdates": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers (e.g., context7): use `claude mcp add --scope user --transport stdio name -- command` which writes to `~/.claude.json` under `mcpServers`. Do NOT edit `~/.claude/mcp.json` for Claude Code MCP — that file is Pilot's config and serves a different system. The two files must not be conflated.
   255→

```json
{
  "project": "cohezion",
  "title": "Move asyncio.Lock from class-level to __init__ to prevent event-loop stale lock in tests",
  "context": "ResourceMonitor had `_lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()` as a class-level attribute. This lock is created at import time, bound to the initial event loop. When pytest creates fresh async event loops per test, the stale class-level lock causes 12+ ERROR failures with RuntimeError. Tests pass individually but fail in full suite.",
  "decision": "Remove `_lock` from class attributes. Add `self._lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()` inside `__init__`, alongside the existing `self.semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore()`. Both are now fresh per singleton instantiation and bound to the active event loop.",
  "rationale": "asyncio.Lock objects are bound to the event loop that creates them. Class-level creation at import time means the lock is bound to whatever event loop existed at module import. Pytest creates new event loops per async test, causing stale lock errors. Instance-level creation in __init__ guarantees the lock is fresh for the current event loop, and the singleton reset fixture (ResourceMonitor._instance = None) ensures a new lock is created per test.",
  "alternatives_considered": "Alternative: Use asyncio.get_event_loop() in a property to lazily create a fresh lock. Rejected: more complex, no benefit over __init__ approach since singleton reset already clears state."
}
```

id: toolu_01XspuCP4FSi8SXJEURohGtm
```json
{
  "source_path": "src/cohezion/reliability/monitor.py",
  "pattern_name": "Async Singleton Lock Isolation — asyncio.Lock in __init__ not class-level",
  "description": "When a singleton class uses asyncio.Lock for thread-safety, the lock MUST be created in __init__, not as a class-level attribute. Class-level asyncio primitives (Lock, Semaphore, Event) are bound to the event loop at class definition time (import time). Pytest creates fresh event loops per async test session, causing RuntimeError on the stale lock. The singleton reset fixture (ClassName._instance = None) ensures __init__ is called again with the new event loop.",
  "code_example": "```python\n# BAD - lock bound to event loop at import time\nclass MySingleton:\n    _instance = None\n    _lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()  # Created once at class definition!\n\n# GOOD - lock fresh per instantiation, bound to active event loop\nclass MySingleton:\n    _instance = None\n    # No asyncio primitives at class level\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        if hasattr(self, '_initialized') and self._initialized:\n            return\n        self._lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()     # Fresh per __init__\n        self.semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(4)  # Fresh per __init__\n        self._initialized = True\n\n# Test fixture must reset singleton to force __init__ re-run\n@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)\ndef reset_singleton():\n    MySingleton._instance = None\n    yield\n    MySingleton._instance = None\n```",
  "domain": "testing"
}
```

```json
{
  "source_path": "tests/unit/test_emergency_shutdown_logic.py",
  "pattern_name": "AsyncMock for Subprocess in Tests — prevent curl/network hangs",
  "description": "When production code calls asyncio.create_subprocess_exec (e.g., curl to an external service), tests that don't mock it will hang if the service is unavailable. Always mock asyncio.create_subprocess_exec in tests that trigger code paths touching external services. The mock process object needs a communicate() method as AsyncMock.",
  "code_example": "```python\n# Production code that may hang in tests:\n# process = await asyncio.create_subprocess_exec(\"curl\", \"-s\", url, ...)\n# stdout, _ = await asyncio.wait_for(process.communicate(), timeout=8.0)\n\n# Test fix - mock the subprocess to avoid real network calls:\nmock_proc = MagicMock()\nmock_proc.communicate = AsyncMock(return_value=(b'{\"models\": []}', b\"\"))\n\nwith patch(\"asyncio.create_subprocess_exec\", return_value=mock_proc):\n    await monitor.emergency_shutdown(vitals)\n\n# Also: AsyncMock (not MagicMock) for semaphore/lock acquire:\nmonitor.semaphore = MagicMock()\nmonitor.semaphore.acquire = AsyncMock(return_value=None)  # Not: side_effect=asyncio.Future!\n\n# Production code: add timeouts to all subprocess calls:\nprocess = await asyncio.create_subprocess_exec(\n    \"curl\", \"-s\", \"--max-time\", \"5\", \"--connect-timeout\", \"3\", url, ...\n)\nstdout, _ = await asyncio.wait_for(process.communicate(), timeout=8.0)\n```",
  "domain": "testing"
}
```

```json
{
  "path": "decisions/2026-02-22-asyncio-lock-class-level-to-init.md",
  "content": "# Decision: Move asyncio.Lock from class-level to __init__\n\n**Date**: 2026-02-22\n**Project**: [[cohezion]]\n**Status**: Implemented\n\n## Context\n\n`ResourceMonitor` had `_lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()` as a class-level attribute. This lock is created at import time, bound to the initial event loop. When pytest creates fresh async event loops per test, the stale class-level lock causes 12+ ERROR failures. Tests pass individually but fail in the full suite.\n\n## Decision\n\nRemove `_lock` from class attributes. Add `self._lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()` inside `__init__`, alongside `self.semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore()`. Both are now fresh per singleton instantiation.\n\n## Rationale\n\nasyncio.Lock objects are bound to the event loop that creates them. Class-level creation at import time means the lock is bound to whatever event loop existed at module import. Pytest creates new event loops per async test group, causing stale lock errors. Instance-level creation in `__init__` guarantees freshness. The singleton reset fixture (`ResourceMonitor._instance = None`) ensures `__init__` is called again per test.\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n- Lazy property that creates a new lock per active event loop — rejected as more complex with no benefit\n- Storing lock as `Optional[asyncio.Lock] = None` — equivalent but less explicit\n\n## Related\n\n- [[patterns/async-singleton-lock-isolation]]\n- [[sessions/session-70-heal-and-test-fix]]\n- Learning 130 in KEY_LEARNINGS.md\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "path": "decisions/2026-02-22-pytestmark-asyncio-module-level.md",
  "content": "# Decision: Use pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio at Module Level\n\n**Date**: 2026-02-22\n**Project**: [[cohezion]]\n**Status**: Implemented\n\n## Context\n\n`test_specifications.py` had 56 `async def test_*` functions with no `@pytest.mark.asyncio`. With `asyncio_mode=strict` in `pytest.ini`, all async tests must be explicitly marked. All 56 failed with \"async def functions are not natively supported\".\n\n## Decision\n\nAdd `pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio` at module level (after imports). This applies the mark to all tests in the module automatically.\n\n## Rationale\n\nModule-level `pytestmark` is a standard pytest pattern. It eliminates 56 individual decorators, ensures no async test is ever missed, and is easy to audit. The single sync test in the file gets a harmless \"asyncio mark on non-async function\" warning which is acceptable.\n\n## Enforcement\n\nNew testing rule added to `.claude/rules/testing.md`: Files with majority async tests should use module-level `pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio` rather than per-function decorators.\n\n## Related\n\n- [[decisions/2026-02-22-asyncio-lock-class-level-to-init]]\n- [[patterns/async-mock-subprocess-in-tests]]\n- Learning 132 in KEY_LEARNINGS.md\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "path": "patterns/async-singleton-lock-isolation.md",
  "content": "# Pattern: Async Singleton Lock Isolation\n\n**Domain**: testing, async\n**Source**: [[src/cohezion/reliability/monitor.py]]\n**Discovered**: Session 70 — 2026-02-22\n\n## Problem\n\nSingleton classes with `asyncio.Lock` as a class-level attribute fail in pytest suites because:\n1. Lock is created at import time, bound to the initial event loop\n2. pytest-asyncio creates fresh event loops per test\n3. Using the stale lock in a new event loop raises `RuntimeError: Attached to a different loop`\n\n## Solution\n\nMove all asyncio primitives out of class-level and into `__init__`. Combine with a singleton reset fixture that nulls `_instance` so `__init__` runs again per test.\n\n```python\n# BAD — lock bound to event loop at import time\nclass ResourceMonitor:\n    _instance = None\n    _lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()  # ❌ Created at class definition!\n\n# GOOD — lock fresh per instantiation\nclass ResourceMonitor:\n    _instance = None\n    # No asyncio primitives at class level\n\n    def __init__(self, max_concurrency: int = 4):\n        if hasattr(self, '_initialized') and self._initialized:\n            return\n        self._lock: asyncio.Lock = asyncio.Lock()          # ✅ Fresh per __init__\n        self.semaphore: asyncio.Semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(max_concurrency)  # ✅ Fresh\n        self._initialized = True\n\n# Test fixture MUST reset singleton so __init__ re-runs:\n@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)\ndef reset_singleton():\n    ResourceMonitor._instance = None\n    yield\n    ResourceMonitor._instance = None\n```\n\n## Detection\n\nSymptom: Tests pass individually but fail with `RuntimeError` or become ERROR in full suite. The error only appears in async test contexts.\n\n## Related\n\n- [[decisions/2026-02-22-asyncio-lock-class-level-to-init]]\n- [[patterns/async-mock-subprocess-in-tests]]\n"
}
```
