   224→## Learning 136: Workflow CLI Migration — Clean-Room Abstraction (2026-02-23)
   225→When a proprietary CLI tool provides workflow automation (session management, worktree isolation, context monitoring, plan registration), it can be replaced by a clean-room implementation that observes the tool's behavior without reading its source. Key approach: (1) map every CLI command to an observable output format, (2) implement using only public APIs (git, filesystem, env vars), (3) replace global rules files to point to new CLI, (4) migrate MCP servers directly to `~/.claude/mcp.json`. The behavior contract is fully specified by the help text and JSON output formats — no source access needed. `cz` replaced `pilot` across all 3 rule files, settings.json, and MCP config in a single session.
   226→
   227→## Learning 137: Subprocess Context JSONL Discovery — Two-Strategy Fallback (2026-02-23)
   228→Claude Code session JSONL files are stored at `~/.claude/projects/<cwd-slug>/*.jsonl`. When running as a subprocess hook, the working directory may differ from the directory Claude Code is actually running in, causing slug mismatch and UNKNOWN context estimates. Fix: (1) try cwd-slug first (fast, exact), (2) fall back to `~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl` sorted by mtime, taking the most recently modified globally. Strategy 2 picks up the active session reliably without any env var injection. Cost: one `rglob()` call per hook invocation (negligible on modern filesystems).
   229→
   230→## Learning 128: Daily CLI Tool Update Pattern (2026-02-22)
   231→Automate CLI tool version checks via systemd timer (daily at 3am). Each tool needs a dedicated update function: (1) read current version, (2) fetch latest from GitHub API or tool's own update command, (3) skip if equal, (4) update and log if different. Tools: `entire` → install.sh, `sx` → `sx update`, `uv` → `uv self update`, `gh` → GitHub release tar.gz download. Systemd `Persistent=true` catches missed runs on next boot. First run caught: uv v0.9.25→v0.10.4, gh v2.62→v2.87, sx v0.10→v0.11.
   232→
   233→## Learning 129: Post-Crash Venv Recovery — pytest Missing Despite pyproject.toml (2026-02-22)
   234→System crashes can corrupt `.venv` without touching `pyproject.toml`. Symptom: `error: Failed to spawn: pytest / No such file or directory` even though pytest is in dev deps. Recovery: (1) `uv sync`, (2) if still failing: `uv add pytest pytest-cov pytest-asyncio --dev`, (3) verify `uv run python -m pytest --collect-only -q`. Requires pytest-cov (for `--cov` addopts in pytest.ini) and pytest-asyncio (for `asyncio_mode=strict`).
   235→
   236→## Learning 130: asyncio.Lock at Class Level = Stale Event Loop Trap (2026-02-22)
   237→`asyncio.Lock()` and `asyncio.Semaphore()` created at class definition time are bound to the initial event loop. pytest-asyncio creates fresh event loops per test group, causing RuntimeError or silent ERRORs in the full suite (tests pass individually). Fix: move all asyncio primitives to `__init__`. Combine with a singleton reset fixture (`ClassName._instance = None`) so `__init__` re-runs per test with a fresh lock. See [[patterns/async-singleton-lock-isolation]].
   238→
   239→## Learning 131: Private-to-Public Method Rename Drift (2026-02-22)
   240→When renaming `_private` → `public` methods, internal call sites in the same file are commonly missed. Session 68 renamed `_holographic_project` → `holographic_project` but line 408 of the same file still called `self._holographic_project()`. Always grep `src/` AND `tests/` after any rename. Python's "Did you mean: 'method_name'?" error message is the detection signal.
   241→
   242→## Learning 132: Module-Level pytestmark for Async Test Files (2026-02-22)
   243→With `asyncio_mode=strict`, async test files without `@pytest.mark.asyncio` silently fail with "async def functions are not natively supported". Fix: add `pytestmark = pytest.mark.asyncio` at module level for files with >50% async tests. Bulk fix: one line beats 56 individual decorators. Mixed files that also have sync tests get a harmless warning.
   244→
   245→## Learning 133: subprocess.communicate Hangs Without Timeout (2026-02-22)
   246→`await process.communicate()` on a subprocess calling an unavailable network service (Ollama, SurrealDB) hangs indefinitely. Fix in production: add `--max-time 5 --connect-timeout 3` to curl args + `asyncio.wait_for(process.communicate(), timeout=8.0)`. Fix in tests: `patch("asyncio.create_subprocess_exec", return_value=mock_proc)` where `mock_proc.communicate = AsyncMock(return_value=(b'{}', b""))`.
   247→
   248→## Learning 134: asyncio.Future as side_effect Anti-Pattern (2026-02-22)
   249→`MagicMock(side_effect=asyncio.Future)` creates a new unresolved Future per call. `await mock.acquire()` then hangs forever waiting for `.set_result()` that never comes. Fix: use `AsyncMock(return_value=None)` for async methods that should complete immediately. The `side_effect=asyncio.Future` pattern is a silent indefinite hang, not a visible failure.
   250→
   251→## Learning 135: Heal-Before-Test Protocol (2026-02-22)
   252→/heal (format + lint fix + __init__.py) must run BEFORE investigating test failures. Formatting changes can fix test collection errors; lint auto-fixes can unblock import errors. Session 70: 83 FAILED + 15 ERROR decomposed into only 6 independent root causes. Pattern: run individually first (all pass = isolation issue, not logic bug), then categorize by root cause, then fix categories in parallel.
   253→
   254→---
   255→
   256→## Session 58: Cosmic Fire Module (2026-02-20)
   257→
   258→### Learning 122: Integration Theater Detection
   259→Initial claims of integration into lcsp.py, morphospace.py, journey_tracker.py were false - fields existed in documentation but not in actual files. Fix: adversarial audit pattern that imports and inspects actual classes rather than trusting edit claims. Pattern: `assert hasattr(Class, 'claimed_field'), "INTEGRATION THEATER"`.
   260→
   261→### Learning 123: Circular Import Resolution via Lazy Loading
   262→JourneyTracker cannot import ThreeFiresEngine at module level due to import cycles. Fix: lazy loading with caching: `_cache = None; def _get_engine(): global _cache; if _cache is None: from path import Engine; _cache = Engine(); return _cache`. This pattern applies to any module that creates cycles when importing from child packages.
   263→
   264→### Learning 124: HIHO Consistency Enforcement
   265→Multiple files had inconsistent HIHO calculations: old pattern `1.0 - abs(c - 0.5)` (linear penalty, wrong shape) vs correct `HihoVectorEngine.calculate_hiho_score(c)` (Gaussian peak at 0.5). Rule: always use shared engine classes, never inline physics calculations.
   266→
   267→### Learning 125: Fire-Type Sigma Tuning Maps to Task Profile
   268→Three Fires have different sigma values mapping to agent task profiles: Electric (σ=0.20, sharp peak) for precision/validation tasks; Solar (σ=0.25, standard) for general work; Friction (σ=0.35, wide) for resilient/recovery tasks. This enables semantic routing based on reliability requirements.
   269→
   270→### Learning 126: 5-Essential-Tests Pattern
   271→Session 58 cosmic module: 34 tests covering 6 modules in 7.7s. Effective test strategy: manual validation → 5 essential tests (happy path, edge-empty, edge-max, error-case, integration-point) → ship. Anti-pattern: 600 pre-build tests with no implementation.
   272→
   273→---
   274→
   275→## Session 59: Dev Environment Recovery (2026-02-20)
   276→
   277→### Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution
   278→When `claude update` warns "Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `"autoUpdates": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers: `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the global MCP config for Claude Code; `~/.claude.json` (`mcpServers`) is a secondary location. Both work; `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the preferred path for programmatic management.
   279→

For user-scope MCP servers: `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the global MCP config for Claude Code; `~/.claude.json` (`mcpServers`) is a secondary location. Both work; `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the preferred path for programmatic management.",
  "new_string": "### Learning 127: Claude Code Native Install vs npm Conflict Resolution\nWhen `claude update` warns \"Running native installation but config install method is 'npm'\": (1) remove leftover npm global: `npm -g uninstall @anthropic-ai/claude-code`, (2) re-run `claude update` which self-corrects `installMethod` in `~/.claude.json`. Root cause of lost auto-updates: `\"autoUpdates\": false` in `~/.claude.json` — fix by setting `true`. For user-scope MCP servers: `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the global MCP config for Claude Code; `~/.claude.json` (`mcpServers`) is a secondary location. Both work; `~/.claude/mcp.json` is the preferred path for programmatic management.\n\n---\n\n## Learning 138: TC001 Unsafe-Fix Breaks Test Patching (2026-02-23)\n`ruff --unsafe-fixes` applies TC001/TC002/TC003 which moves imports inside `if TYPE_CHECKING:` blocks. Any test that patches a name via `patch(\"module.ClassName\")` then fails with `AttributeError: module does not have attribute 'ClassName'` — because TYPE_CHECKING imports are not present in the module namespace at runtime. Detection: tests that pass before `--unsafe-fixes` fail afterward. Fix: add `\"TC001\", \"TC002\", \"TC003\"` to the ruff `ignore` list in `pyproject.toml`. Alternatively, for specific cases, move the import back to top-level and add `# noqa: TC001`.\n\n## Learning 139: pyproject.toml Curated Ignore List for ML/Simulation Codebases (2026-02-23)\nML/simulation codebases trigger many ruff security rules as false positives. Better to add a project-level ignore list than scatter thousands of per-line `# noqa` comments. Categories for Cohezion: `S311` (random for simulation, not security), `S603/S607` (subprocess in infrastructure scripts, legitimate), `N806` (uppercase variables X/Y/Z are conventional in physics/ML code), `S108` (temp files in test helpers, not production), `S608` (SurrealDB queries are parameterized — not traditional SQL injection). Process: (1) run `ruff check src/ --statistics` to identify false-positive categories by count, (2) verify each category is genuinely non-security before ignoring globally, (3) set line-length to 120 for scientific code with long type hints. Result: 831 → 203 errors (75.6% reduction) without losing meaningful coverage."
}
```

Session 72 is complete. All three outstanding tasks are done:

## Recommended Next Steps (Priority Order)

### 1. Fix RUF012 — 43 real bugs (mutable class defaults)
These are actual logic bugs where dataclass/Pydantic fields use mutable defaults directly:
```python
# Bug: shared mutable state across instances
field: list = []
# Fix:
field: list = field(default_factory=list)
```
Run `uv run ruff check src/cohezion/ --select RUF012` to see the list. This is straightforward mechanical work.

### 2. Fix RUF006 — 15 asyncio dangling task bugs
Tasks created without storing a reference get garbage-collected silently:
```python
# Bug: task can be GC'd
asyncio.create_task(some_coroutine())
# Fix:
self._tasks.add(asyncio.create_task(some_coroutine()))
```
Run `uv run ruff check src/cohezion/ --select RUF006`.

### 3. Log vault entries for L138/L139
The two new KEY_LEARNINGS entries from this session should be persisted to `~/vaults/cohezion-vault/patterns/` with bidirectional links so they survive across contexts.

### 4. mem-search MCP replacement
Lower priority — no current operational blocker, but cross-session memory search is missing since PILOT was removed.

---

**Suggested start**: `/test-fix` after tackling RUF012 to ensure no regressions, or just run `uv run ruff check src/cohezion/ --select RUF012,RUF006 --fix` as a dry-run first to see the scope.
